Use when asked to fill missing or deferred capabilities, compare upstream or legacy implementations, salvage prior art, centralize source or test declarations, eliminate nested functions or superfluous wrappers, maximize declared @orkestrel dependenciesâ€”especially @orkestrel/contractâ€”or add rigorous real-implementation and live-service tests. Select only the phases required by a narrow request; run the full workflow for production readiness or comprehensive hardening.\r\n---\r\n\r\n# Harden an Orkestrel package\r\n\r\n## Load authority\r\n\r\nRead the current files in this order:\r\n\r\n1. `AGENTS.md`.\r\n2. Every applicable `.claude/rules/*.md`.\r\n3. Select the work lane below and read every reference that lane requires.\r\n4. `guides/README.md`, the governing package/domain guide, and `ROADMAP.md` when present.\r\n5. The authoritative `*/types.ts`, public barrels, `package.json`, build/test configuration, and decision-bearing implementation files.\r\n\r\nTreat the current user instruction as authoritative. Treat repository rules as the coding contract and this skill as the workflow. Preserve dirty and user-owned work.\r\n\r\nThe decision owner must read the governing types and every implementation file the user names directly. Delegate bulk reconnaissance or supporting research, not the final design decision.\r\n\r\n## Select the work lane\r\n\r\nChoose the smallest lane that fully satisfies the request:\r\n\r\n- **Structural:** centralization, nested-function removal, wrapper cleanup, or test-infrastructure consolidation.\r\n- **Capability:** research and implement named missing behavior or upstream parity.\r\n- **Hardening:** perform the complete production-readiness workflow.\r\n\r\nA narrow structural request does not authorize unrelated API redesign. A hardening request does authorize fixing every verified in-scope defect and completing every in-scope deferred capability.\r\n\r\nLoad [research.md](references/research.md) for upstream comparisons, legacy/`old/` material, or capability discovery.\r\n\r\nLoad [contract.md](references/contract.md) whenever the package declares `@orkestrel/contract`, the user requests contract adoption, or local code overlaps validation, parsing, outcomes, safe exception capture, schemas, or contract generation.\r\n\r\nLoad [centralization.md](references/centralization.md) for every structural lane and as a mandatory cleanup pass after capability or hardening work.\r\n\r\nLoad [hardening.md](references/hardening.md) for the hardening lane and for any request involving concurrency, lifecycle pressure, security boundaries, real services/models, test discovery, or package inspection.\r\n\r\n## Execute the workflow\r\n\r\n1. **Bound the campaign.** Record requested outcomes, in-scope environments/domains, explicit exclusions, supported hosts, dirty files, and evidence needed for acceptance.\r\n2. **Map before editing.** Trace public types, implementations, callers, tests, guides, exports, runtime boundaries, installed Orkestrel dependencies, and applicable legacy/upstream references.\r\n3. **Establish the intended contract.** Build a capability/defect matrix. Separate verified fact from inference. Mark each row implement, repair, retain, or exclude with a reason. The matrix is fixed at this step and is the campaign's definition of done: every later step serves a row, and work that serves no row belongs to the next campaign. The row set is fixed; the planned work that closes it is not. Re-baseline that work at each phase boundary per `.agents/orchestration.md`, which owns the step and its boundary with rescoping.\r\n4. **Design types first.** Update guide/spec intent and `*/types.ts` before implementation, under the root design laws. A contract that needs a compatibility shim is the wrong contract.\r\n5. **Implement completely.** Finish every in-scope branch and reuse the exact installed Orkestrel primitives whose semantics match. The root completion law decides what may not be left behind.\r\n6. **Prove each defect before repairing it.** A repair begins with a test that fails for that defect: record the exact command and its failing count before the fix and the same command's passing count after. A repair with no red-then-green record is unproven.\r\n7. **Consolidate.** Run the complete centralization and wrapper sweep. Update all call sites to the real symbol rather than leaving aliases or 1:1 delegates.\r\n8. **Challenge seams.** Add deterministic tests for invariants, boundaries, failures, lifecycle, cleanup, cancellation, concurrency, hostile input, and resource pressure as applicable, under the test rules' real-implementation law.\r\n9. **Use live services deliberately.** Put real external services/models in their dedicated project, require readiness, and make each request minimally sufficient, stable across the service's nondeterminism, and behaviorally meaningful. When the claim is that a foreign client can use this package, drive one representative real client end to end.\r\n10. **Document the final behavior.** Update the governing guide, examples, method tables, limitations, and parity coverage. Document architectural limits honestly.\r\n11. **Audit completion.** Inspect test discovery, `.todo`/`.skip`/conditional skip use, source/test helper duplication, exports, environment isolation, unexpected text corruption, and the entire diff.\r\n12. **Verify.** Run the repository-prescribed gates in order and inspect the generated outputs relevant to the request.\r\n13. **Review independently, and never by the author.** When orchestration is available, run the adversarial pass â€” subjective design fit and objective correctness â€” plus a mechanical checker, per `.agents/orchestration.md`. Add a dedicated adversarial round for security, concurrency, destructive paths, or external input. A unit's auditor is an engine that did not write it; same-engine re-review returns the author's own blind spot. Resolve every required finding, then rerun affected verification.\r\n\r\n## Accept the result\r\n\r\nDo not accept â€œmostly complete,â€ â€œtests passâ€ without adequacy review, or a builder's self-report. Completion requires:\r\n\r\n- all requested capabilities implemented or explicitly proven out of scope;\r\n- red-then-green evidence for every repaired defect;\r\n- public contract, implementation, tests, guides, and generated outputs aligned;\r\n- no unresolved centralization, wrapper, dependency-reuse, test-discovery, lifecycle, security, or text-integrity finding;\r\n- exact command evidence for every final gate.\r\n\r\nCompletion is those conditions met, not the absence of anything further to find. When every row is closed and the gates are green, stopping is the correct action and the next campaign is the deliverable; a further pass over the same surface is a new instruction from the user, not a finding.\r\n\r\nReport what changed, what evidence proves it, exact test/gate results, and any genuine residual risk. Do not call an in-scope omission â€œfuture work.â€\r\n# Adopt `@orkestrel/contract`\r\n\r\n## Inspect the exact installed package\r\n\r\nNever work from a remembered contract version or copied API list. Read:\r\n\r\n1. `package.json` and the lockfile for the declared and resolved versions.\r\n2. The vendored `guides/contract.md` when present.\r\n3. The installed package's declaration file and exports.\r\n4. Relevant package source or current canonical guide when semantics remain unclear.\r\n\r\nDo not add `@orkestrel/contract` unless the user authorizes a dependency change. When it is already declared, use it to its full semantically appropriate extent.\r\n\r\n## Build an overlap matrix\r\n\r\nInventory local code involving:\r\n\r\n- primitive, structural, instance, collection, and total guards;\r\n- guard combinators and refinements;\r\n- coercing parsers and field readers;\r\n- JSON parsing and narrowing;\r\n- `Result`, success/failure outcomes, and safe exception capture;\r\n- contract shapes, compiled guards/parsers/schemas/generators/reporters;\r\n- finite/integer checks, error narrowing, and hostile-boundary containment.\r\n\r\nFor each local symbol, record:\r\n\r\n| Local symbol | Contract candidate | Same semantics? | Difference | Decision | Required tests |\r\n| ------------ | ------------------ | --------------- | ---------- | -------- | -------------- |\r\n\r\nReplace only when semantics match or the current contract primitive is intentionally better for the desired API. A similar name is not proof.\r\n\r\n## Refactor without wrappers\r\n\r\n- Import the originating contract symbol directly when it already expresses the required operation.\r\n- Update downstream call sites atomically.\r\n- Delete rename-only, pass-through, and compatibility wrappers.\r\n- Keep a local helper only when it adds a real invariant, composition, projection, domain translation, or boundary.\r\n- Do not re-export contract symbols from another package's barrel.\r\n- Prefer one compiled contract over independently maintained guard/parser/schema logic when the package genuinely needs those outputs.\r\n- Prefer contract outcomes and safe attempt boundaries over repeated ad hoc `try`/`catch` shapes when their failure semantics match.\r\n\r\nDo not force contract abstractions into plain control flow where they obscure behavior or change the error contract.\r\n\r\n## Prove strictness and soundness\r\n\r\nTest every adopted boundary for relevant invariants:\r\n\r\n- guards are total for adversarial values and hostile prototypes;\r\n- parsed values satisfy the paired guard;\r\n- guard-valid values retain identity when promised;\r\n- invalid or ambiguous values fail in the documented form;\r\n- caught non-`Error` throws normalize as documented;\r\n- compiled schema, parser, guard, generator, and reporter stay aligned when used;\r\n- finite, integer, `NaN`, infinities, signed zero, cycles, depth, and cross-realm behavior are covered where applicable.\r\n\r\nRun the centralization sweep after replacement so obsolete local types, helpers, constants, and tests do not remain.\r\n# Centralization and simplification\r\n\r\nPlacement, kind purity, the wrapper test, the no-nested-function law, and barrel law live\r\nin `.claude/rules/architecture.md`; shared test infrastructure and helper placement live in\r\n`.claude/rules/tests.md`. This reference adds only the sweep those laws assume: what to\r\ninventory, how to classify, and what must be proven before acceptance.\r\n\r\n## Inventory what you touched\r\n\r\nInspect every touched implementation and centralized file, then sweep the full affected\r\nenvironments.\r\n\r\n| Where               | Inventory                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |\r\n| ------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\r\n| Implementation file | Interfaces and type aliases, module constants and data, free functions/guards/parsers/factories/schemas, function declarations or assignments inside bodies, extra classes, imports and exports stranded by a move                                            |\r\n| Centralized file    | Every module declaration: does it match that file's kind, is it exported, is every intentional top-level export reachable from the correct environment barrel regardless of current consumers, does it carry direct behavioral coverage when it carries logic |\r\n| Environment root    | Every declaration promoted there: at least two consuming modules, or it belongs to the owning module's centralized file                                                                                                                                       |\r\n\r\nThe rare runtime-self-contained entrypoint exception must be literally required for\r\nexecution and must explain why sibling imports cannot work.\r\n\r\n## Classify every function\r\n\r\n| Signal                                     | Home                                                 |\r\n| ------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------- |\r\n| Reaches instance state or a sibling method | Class method                                         |\r\n| Pure self-contained computation            | Exported centralized helper, parser, compiler, guard |\r\n| Defining recursive or compositional spine  | Class method, after extracting its pure leaves       |\r\n| Trivial and genuinely one-use              | Inline it into the caller                            |\r\n\r\nNever move logic into a nested function to evade centralization. An anonymous callback\r\npassed directly to another operation stays a callback, not a hidden helper declaration.\r\n\r\n## Hunt the wrapper\r\n\r\nSearch callers and callees for one-line delegates, pass-through factories, getters that\r\nrename another public getter, duplicate guards or parsers a declared dependency already\r\nsupplies, compatibility aliases and re-exports, and functions whose only purpose is\r\navoiding a downstream rename. The architecture rules' wrapper test decides each one; a\r\nsurvivor owns a real boundary, invariant, composition, translation, lifecycle, or\r\nmaterially narrower contract. Otherwise use or rename the real symbol and update every\r\nconsumer.\r\n\r\nDo not hollow a class into public methods that each forward to one helper.\r\n\r\n## Consolidate tests\r\n\r\nSweep test files for repeated or reusable input and result records, builders and\r\nfactories, recorders and event capture, wait and readiness helpers, temporary workspace or\r\nfixture-server setup, browser and DOM builders, event factories, and service request\r\nbuilders or response assertions. Move each into the setup file its environment owns.\r\n\r\nAdd focused tests for every exported function extracted from production code. Do not create\r\nisolated tests for declaration-only types, constants, barrels, or error definitions.\r\n\r\n## Prove the sweep\r\n\r\nBefore acceptance, prove:\r\n\r\n- no stray declarations remain in implementation files;\r\n- no non-exported or wrong-kind declaration remains in centralized files;\r\n- no prohibited nested function declaration or assignment remains;\r\n- no duplicate or rename-only wrapper remains;\r\n- every move updated imports, barrels, guides, and tests;\r\n- test helpers are consolidated without over-generalizing one-off setup;\r\n- files are valid UTF-8 with no replacement characters, mojibake, unintended control\r\n  characters, or accidental trailing debris.\r\n\r\nReview the complete diff after formatting. Formatting cannot substitute for the structural\r\nsweep.\r\n# Production hardening\r\n\r\n## Build the risk and seam matrix\r\n\r\nList every boundary where assumptions can fail:\r\n\r\n- public inputs and option combinations;\r\n- empty, missing, minimum, maximum, overflow, `NaN`, infinity, and signed-zero values;\r\n- invalid state transitions and repeated lifecycle operations;\r\n- partial work, abort, timeout, retry, pause/resume, stop/destroy, and cleanup;\r\n- concurrency, reentrancy, ordering, idempotency, fairness, and backpressure;\r\n- filesystem, process, socket, protocol, browser, model, and network boundaries;\r\n- hostile strings, paths, headers, frames, JSON, prototypes, getters, and proxies;\r\n- resource ownership, leaks, teardown, and pressure;\r\n- environment isolation and unsupported-host behavior;\r\n- serialization, restore, public exports, and consumer ergonomics.\r\n\r\nFor each applicable seam, state the invariant, failure mode, observable result, and proving test. Omit irrelevant generic cases rather than creating meaningless tests.\r\n\r\n## Use real implementations\r\n\r\nTest through public behavior with:\r\n\r\n| Seam                                    | The real thing it must be driven with                                    |\r\n| --------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------ |\r\n| Package behavior                        | Real package classes and composed managers                               |\r\n| Filesystem                              | Temporary directories and files                                          |\r\n| Network protocol                        | Actual local sockets and protocol-faithful fixture servers               |\r\n| Browser                                 | An actual Chromium-family browser when the browser is the system         |\r\n| Declared dependency                     | The actual installed dependency                                          |\r\n| External service or model               | The real service in its dedicated project when its behavior is the claim |\r\n| Third-party client or protocol consumer | One representative real foreign client, driven end to end                |\r\n\r\nA third-party seam is the one case where the package's own tests cannot close the claim:\r\nprotocol tests prove the protocol, and only a real client of that class proves the\r\nintegration. Drive it end to end, record the exact commands, and record what that client\r\ncould NOT reach as an honest limit rather than an untested assumption.\r\n\r\nNever use mocks, behavioral fakes, module replacement, or framework spies. Use recorders for callbacks and customizable data factories/stubs for inert shapes.\r\n\r\n## Design live-service tests\r\n\r\nKeep live tests in a dedicated project with explicit readiness, setup, timeout, and cleanup.\r\n\r\n- Require the real service; fail loudly when unavailable.\r\n- Never silently skip or convert absence into a passing result.\r\n- Warm the service before measured assertions when startup is material.\r\n- Use the smallest prompt/request/context that proves one behavior.\r\n- Make assertions semantic and bounded, not dependent on exact prose.\r\n- For model tests, constrain temperature/seed/options when the real API supports it, but do not claim determinism the provider does not promise.\r\n- Increase context or workload incrementally only when the scenario requires it.\r\n- Test instruction precedence, long-context behavior, summarization, tool calls, scopes, and state transitions through observable outcomes.\r\n- Avoid redundant expensive calls; one request proves one primary claim.\r\n\r\nKeep live projects outside the fast default suite when repository policy requires it, while making their explicit command authoritative for the campaign.\r\n\r\n## Challenge lifecycle and pressure\r\n\r\nExercise:\r\n\r\n- repeated start/stop/pause/resume/abort/destroy calls;\r\n- operations during transitions and after teardown;\r\n- concurrent managers/entities and interleaved completion;\r\n- late events, listener errors, rejection paths, and cleanup after partial setup;\r\n- append/change/remove behavior while work is active versus already advanced;\r\n- queues, pools, workers, terminals, sockets, or browsers at representative concurrency;\r\n- cancellation propagation and bounded shutdown;\r\n- no busy polling, open The domain conversation and call configuration\r\n\t * @returns The validated wire request with caller context omitted\r\n\t * @throws ProviderError Thrown when the projected request is not JSON\r\n\t */\r\n\tbody(request: ProviderRequest): object {\r\n\t\tconst projected = {\r\n\t\t\tmessages: request.messages.map((message) => ({\r\n\t\t\t\tid: message.id,\r\n\t\t\t\trole: message.role,\r\n\t\t\t\tcontent: message.content,\r\n\t\t\t\t...(message.calls === undefined\r\n\t\t\t\t\t? {}\r\n\t\t\t\t\t: {\r\n\t\t\t\t\t\t\tcalls: message.calls.map((call) => ({\r\n\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\tid: call.id,\r\n\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\tname: call.name,\r\n\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\targuments: call.arguments,\r\n\t\t\t\t\t\t\t})),\r\n\t\t\t\t\t\t}),\r\n\t\t\t\t...(message.images === undefined ? {} : { images: message.images }),\r\n\t\t\t})),\r\n\t\t\t...(request.tools === undefined\r\n\t\t\t\t? {}\r\n\t\t\t\t: {\r\n\t\t\t\t\t\ttools: request.tools.map((tool) => ({\r\n\t\t\t\t\t\t\tname: tool.name,\r\n\t\t\t\t\t\t\t...(tool.description === undefined ? {} : { description: tool.description }),\r\n\t\t\t\t\t\t\t...(tool.parameters === undefined ? {} : { parameters: tool.parameters }),\r\n\t\t\t\t\t\t})),\r\n\t\t\t\t\t}),\r\n\t\t\t...(request.options === undefined\r\n\t\t\t\t? {}\r\n\t\t\t\t: {\r\n\t\t\t\t\t\toptions: {\r\n\t\t\t\t\t\t\t...(request.options.think === undefined ? {} : { think: request.options.think }),\r\n\t\t\t\t\t\t\t...(request.options.schema === undefined ? {} : { schema: request.options.schema }),\r\n\t\t\t\t\t\t},\r\n\t\t\t\t\t}),\r\n\t\t}\r\n\t\tif (!providerRequestContract.is(projected)) {\r\n\t\t\tthrow new ProviderError('PROTOCOL', 'relay request is not JSON')\r\n\t\t}\r\n\t\treturn projected\r\n\t}\r\n\r\n\t/**\r\n\t * Validates a relay frame and translates its channel into the shared stream engine.\r\n\t *\r\n\t * @param record - The call's retained framing state\r\n\t * @returns Records completed by the final newline\r\n\t */\r\n\tfinish(parser: ProviderParserInterface): ReadonlyArray<Readonly<Record<string, unknown>>> {\r\n\t\treturn parser.parse('\\n')\r\n\t}\r\n}\r\nimport type { ProviderDelta, ProviderResult, RelayFrame, RelayStreamOptions } from './types.js'\r\nimport { RELAY_CONTENT_TYPE, RELAY_PROVIDER_MESSAGE } from './constants.js'\r\nimport { relayFrameContract } from './contracts.js'\r\nimport { ProviderAbortError, ProviderError } from './errors.js'\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Streams a provider call as validated NDJSON frames under response backpressure.\r\n *\r\n * @remarks\r\n * Cancellation aborts upstream before returning its iterator. Request listeners are\r\n * released on settlement. Unexpected provider failures carry a fixed public message.\r\n *\r\n * @example\r\n * ```ts\r\n * const response = new RelayStream({ provider, request: { messages: [] }, signal }).response\r\n * ```\r\n */\r\nexport class RelayStream {\r\n\treadonly #upstream = new AbortController()\r\n\treadonly #encoder = new TextEncoder()\r\n\treadonly #iterator: AsyncGenerator<ProviderDelta, ProviderResult>\r\n\treadonly #signal: AbortSignal\r\n\treadonly #abort: () => void\r\n\treadonly #response: Response\r\n\t#settled = false\r\n\r\n\tconstructor(options: RelayStreamOptions) {\r\n\t\tthis.#signal = options.signal\r\n\t\tthis.#abort = this.#abortProvider.bind(this)\r\n\t\tthis.#signal.addEventListener('abort', this.#abort, { once: true })\r\n\t\tif (this.#signal.aborted) this.#abortProvider()\r\n\t\tthis.#iterator = options.provider.stream(\r\n\t\t\toptions.request.messages,\r\n\t\t\tthis.#upstream.signal,\r\n\t\t\toptions.request.tools,\r\n\t\t\toptions.request.options,\r\n\t\t)\r\n\t\tthis.#response = new Response(\r\n\t\t\tnew ReadableStream<Uint8Array>({\r\n\t\t\t\tpull: this.#pull.bind(this),\r\n\t\t\t\tcancel: this.#cancel.bind(this),\r\n\t\t\t}),\r\n\t\t\t{ headers: { 'content-type': RELAY_CONTENT_TYPE, 'cache-control': 'no-store' } },\r\n\t\t)\r\n\t}\r\n\r\n\t/** Exposes the pull-driven response for the provider call. */\r\n\tget response(): Response {\r\n\t\treturn this.#response\r\n\t}\r\n\r\n\tasync #pull(controller: ReadableStreamDefaultController<Uint8Array>): Promise<void> {\r\n\t\tif (this.#settled) return\r\n\t\ttry {\r\n\t\t\tconst step = await this.#iterator.next()\r\n\t\t\tif (this.#settled) return\r\n\t\t\tthis.#write(controller, step.done ? { channel: 'result', result: step.value } : step.value)\r\n\t\t\tif (step.done) this.#finish(controller)\r\n\t\t} catch (error) {\r\n\t\t\tif (this.#settled) return\r\n\t\t\tconst frame: RelayFrame =\r\n\t\t\t\terror instanceof ProviderAbortError\r\n\t\t\t\t\t? { channel: 'abort', partial: error.partial }\r\n\t\t\t\t\t: { channel: 'error', code: 'PROVIDER', message: RELAY_PROVIDER_MESSAGE }\r\n\t\t\tthis.#write(\r\n\t\t\t\tcontroller,\r\n\t\t\t\trelayFrameContract.is(frame)\r\n\t\t\t\t\t? frame\r\n\t\t\t\t\t: { channel: 'error', code: 'PROVIDER', message: RELAY_PROVIDER_MESSAGE },\r\n\t\t\t)\r\n\t\t\tthis.#finish(controller)\r\n\t\t\tthis.#abortProvider()\r\n\t\t\tawait this.#return()\r\n\t\t}\r\n\t}\r\n\r\n\tasync #cancel(): Promise<void> {\r\n\t\tif (this.#settled) return\r\n\t\tthis.#settled = true\r\n\t\tthis.#abortProvider()\r\n\t\ttry {\r\n\t\t\tawait this.#return()\r\n\t\t} finally {\r\n\t\t\tthis.#release()\r\n\t\t}\r\n\t}\r\n\r\n\tasync #return(): Promise<void> {\r\n\t\ttry {\r\n\t\t\tawait this.#iterator.return({ content: '' })\r\n\t\t} catch {\r\n\t\t\t// A settled response has no error channel for upstream cleanup failures.\r\n\t\t}\r\n\t}\r\n\r\n\t#write(controller: ReadableStreamDefaultController<Uint8Array>, frame: RelayFrame): void {\r\n\t\tif (!relayFrameContract.is(frame)) throw new ProviderError('PROTOCOL', 'invalid relay frame')\r\n\t\tcontroller.enqueue(this.#encoder.encode(`${JSON.stringify(frame)}\\n`))\r\n\t}\r\n\r\n\t#finish(controller: ReadableStreamDefaultController<Uint8Array>): void {\r\n\t\tthis.#settled = true\r\n\t\tthis.#release()\r\n\t\tcontroller.close()\r\n\t}\r\n\r\n\t#abortProvider(): void {\r\n\t\tthis.#upstream.abort(this.#signal.reason)\r\n\t}\r\n\r\n\t#release(): void {\r\n\t\tthis.#signal.removeEventListener('abort', this.#abort)\r\n\t}\r\n}\r\nimport type {\r\n\tAgentContextInterface,\r\n\tAgentContextOptions,\r\n\tAgentInterface,\r\n\tAgentJobInput,\r\n\tAgentOptions,\r\n\tAgentQueueOptions,\r\n\tAgentRegistryInterface,\r\n\tAgentRegistryOptions,\r\n\tAgentResult,\r\n\tAgentRunnerOptions,\r\n\tAuthorityInterface,\r\n\tAuthorityOptions,\r\n\tChannelInterface,\r\n\tConversationInterface,\r\n\tConversationManagerInterface,\r\n\tConversationManagerOptions,\r\n\tConversationOptions,\r\n\tConversationSnapshotRow,\r\n\tConversationStoreInterface,\r\n\tInstructionInput,\r\n\tInstructionInterface,\r\n\tInstructionManagerInterface,\r\n\tInstructionManagerOptions,\r\n\tProviderInterface,\r\n\tRelayHandler,\r\n\tRelayOptions,\r\n\tRelayProviderOptions,\r\n\tScopeInput,\r\n\tScopeInterface,\r\n\tScopeManagerInterface,\r\n\tScopeManagerOptions,\r\n\tThinkSplitterInterface,\r\n} {\r\n\tDEFAULT_RELAY_LIMIT,\r\n\tINVALID_RELAY_STATUS,\r\n\tOVERSIZED_RELAY_STATUS,\r\n\tUNAUTHORIZED_RELAY_STATUS,\r\n} from './constants.js'\r\nimport { providerRequestContract } from './scopes/Scope.js'\r\nimport { ScopeManager } from './scopes/ScopeManager.js'\r\nimport { ThinkSplitter } from './ThinkSplitter.js'\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Creates an authorized relay handler that validates a bounded JSON request before streaming.\r\n *\r\n * @param options - The upstream provider, authorization decision, and optional byte budget\r\n * @returns A fetch-standard handler suitable for a router\r\n * @example\r\n * ```ts\r\n * const handler = createRelay({ provider, authorize: (request) => verify(request) })\r\n * const response = await handler(request)\r\n * ```\r\n */\r\nexport function createRelay(options: RelayOptions): RelayHandler {\r\n\tconst { provider, authorize, limit } = options\r\n\treturn async (request) => {\r\n\t\ttry {\r\n\t\t\tif ((await authorize(request)) !== true) {\r\n\t\t\t\treturn new Response(undefined, { status: UNAUTHORIZED_RELAY_STATUS })\r\n\t\t\t}\r\n\t\t} catch {\r\n\t\t\treturn new Response(undefined, { status: UNAUTHORIZED_RELAY_STATUS })\r\n\t\t}\r\n\t\tif (request.body === null) return new Response(undefined, { status: INVALID_RELAY_STATUS })\r\n\t\tconst read = await readText(request.body, limit ?? DEFAULT_RELAY_LIMIT, request.signal)\r\n\t\tif (!read.complete) return new Response(undefined, { status: OVERSIZED_RELAY_STATUS })\r\n\t\tconst parsed = parseJSONAs(read.text, providerRequestContract.is)\r\n\t\tif (parsed === undefined) return new Response(undefined, { status: INVALID_RELAY_STATUS })\r\n\t\treturn new RelayStream({ provider, request: parsed, signal: request.signal }).response\r\n\t}\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Creates a provider that carries calls through a relay endpoint.\r\n *\r\n * @param options - The endpoint, parser factory, and HTTP call configuration\r\n * @returns The concrete relay provider\r\n * @example\r\n * ```ts\r\n * const provider = createRelayProvider({ url: 'https://relay.example/', parser: createParser })\r\n * ```\r\n */\r\nexport function createRelayProvider(options: RelayProviderOptions): RelayProvider {\r\n\treturn new RelayProvider(options)\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Creates a conversation â€” a {@link ConversationInterface} grouping messages above a flat\r\n * message store it owns directly, with compaction into summarized sections, a regenerated\r\n * rollup `summary`, on-demand `rehydrate`, and substring `search`, driven by a\r\n * provider-agnostic {@link ConversationSummaryHandler} seam.\r\n *\r\n * @remarks\r\n * Append turns through the conversation's own `add` (the live tail it owns); `view()` is the model input\r\n * (each section as a summary message, then the live tail). `compact()` folds the older live\r\n * messages into a summarized {@link Section} and regenerates the rollup â€” it requires\r\n * a `summarize` (omitted â‡’ `compact()` throws a `ConversationError`); `keep` retains a recent\r\n * tail (default `DEFAULT_CONVERSATION_KEEP` â€” fold all). `rehydrate(id)` / `search(query)` read\r\n * the retained originals. Observable (`emitter` â€” `compact` / `summary` / `rehydrate`), wired\r\n * through the reserved `on` option; the emitter isolates a listener throw and routes it to\r\n * its `error` handler (the `error` option), so it can never corrupt a compaction.\r\n *\r\n * @param options - Optional `id` / `on` hooks + the `summarize` seam + `keep` (see {@link ConversationOptions})\r\n * @returns A working {@link ConversationInterface}\r\n *\r\n * @example Conversations & compaction\r\n * ```ts\r\n * import { createConversation } from '@orkestrel/agent'\r\n * import type { ProviderInterface } from '@orkestrel/agent'\r\n *\r\n * declare const provider: ProviderInterface // any concrete implementation supplied by the host app\r\n * // The summarizer seam â€” built from the provider by the runtime; core stays provider-agnostic.\r\n * // Append the instruction as the FINAL user turn: a chat model emits nothing when the prompt\r\n * // ends on an assistant turn, so a leading-system instruction is unreliable.\r\n * const conversation = createConversation({\r\n * \tsummarize: async (messages) =>\r\n * \t\t(\r\n * \t\t\tawait provider.generate(\r\n * \t\t\t\t[\r\n * \t\t\t\t\t...messages,\r\n * \t\t\t\t\t{ id: 's', role: 'user', content: 'Summarize the conversation so far concisely.' },\r\n * \t\t\t\t],\r\n * \t\t\t\tAbortSignal.timeout(30_000),\r\n * \t\t\t)\r\n * \t\t).content,\r\n * \tkeep: 2, // retain the two most recent turns verbatim on each compaction\r\n * })\r\n * conversation.add([\r\n * \t{ role: 'user', content: 'My name is Ada.' },\r\n * \t{ role: 'assistant', content: 'Nice to meet you, Ada.' },\r\n * \t{ role: 'user', content: 'What did I say my name was?' },\r\n * ])\r\n *\r\n * const section = await conversation.compact() // folds the older turns â†’ a summarized section\r\n * conversation.view() // [<section summary message>, ...the retained recent tail] â€” the model input\r\n * conversation.summary // the regenerated rollup (a summary-of-summaries over all sections)\r\n * conversation.search('ada') // case-insensitive across sections' originals + the live tail\r\n * section && conversation.rehydrate(section.id) // the section's full original messages (a pure read)\r\n * ```\r\n */\r\nexport function createConversation(options?: ConversationOptions): ConversationInterface {\r\n\treturn new Conversation(options)\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Creates a conversation registry â€” a {@link ConversationManagerInterface} holding\r\n * {@link ConversationInterface}s keyed by their `id`, in insertion order, with an active pointer:\r\n * the id-keyed store over the conversation layer plus the `active` / `switch` seam the context\r\n * renders. `add` auto-activates the first conversation and flows the registry's default\r\n * `summarize` / `keep` into every conversation it creates.\r\n *\r\n * @remarks\r\n * Starts empty; `add(input?)` mints a {@link ConversationInterface} (its `id` from the input\r\n * or a random UUID), flowing the manager's default `summarize` / `keep` in unless the input\r\n * overrides them, and stores it (an already-present `id` overwrites â€” last write wins) â€” and\r\n * auto-activates the first one (a registry with conversations always has one `active`); a later\r\n * `add` leaves `active` unchanged. `switch(id)` re-points `active` (an unknown `id` returns\r\n * `undefined`, leaving `active` unchanged â€” lenient, never throws); `conversation(id)` /\r\n * `conversations()` look up; `remove` (one or a batch) reports `true` only when every supplied id\r\n * was removed and clears `active` if it was a removed one; `clear` empties it and clears `active`.\r\n * Event-free\r\n * (each conversation owns its own observable `emitter`). A conversation created with neither a\r\n * manager default nor a per-`add` `summarize` cannot `compact` (it throws a `ConversationError`).\r\n *\r\n * @param options - Optional default `summarize` / `keep` (see {@link ConversationManagerOptions})\r\n * @returns An empty {@link ConversationManagerInterface}\r\n *\r\n * @example\r\n * ```ts\r\n * import { createConversationManager } from '@orkestrel/agent'\r\n *\r\n * const conversations = createConversationManager({ summarize: async (m) => `recap of ${m.length}` })\r\n * const chat = conversations.add() // auto-activates â€” conversations.active === chat\r\n * chat.add({ role: 'user', content: 'Hello' })\r\n * ```\r\n */\r\nexport function createConversationManager(\r\n\toptions?: ConversationManagerOptions,\r\n): ConversationManagerInterface {\r\n\treturn new ConversationManager(options)\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Creates the in-memory conversation store â€” a {@link ConversationStoreInterface} backed by a\r\n * process-lifetime `Map` of {@link import('./types.js').ConversationSnapshot}s keyed by conversation\r\n * id, the default backing for the durable {@link ConversationManagerInterface.open} /\r\n * {@link ConversationManagerInterface.save} seam. The exact twin of\r\n * {@link import('@orkestrel/workspace').createMemoryWorkspaceStore}.\r\n *\r\n * @remarks\r\n * A plain `Map` (the snapshot is already pure JSON, so no encoding is needed for the memory tier),\r\n * the structural twin of {@link import('@orkestrel/workspace').createMemoryWorkspaceStore}.\r\n * `get` / `set` / `delete` are async (the\r\n * same shape a durable backend fits); unlike a session store there is no idle-TTL / eviction â€” a\r\n * persisted conversation lives until an explicit `delete`. Its driver-pluggable twin is\r\n * {@link createDatabaseConversationStore} (the snapshot as one opaque JSON column over a `databases`\r\n * table) â€” for a durable store pass it a JSON / SQLite / IndexedDB driver, and it swaps in without\r\n * touching the manager or the conversation. Hydration stays a manager concern: read a snapshot back\r\n * and rebuild the live conversation through the `snapshot` option (re-supplying the live\r\n * `summarize` / `keep`).\r\n *\r\n * @returns A memory-backed {@link ConversationStoreInterface}\r\n *\r\n * @example\r\n * ```ts\r\n * import { createConversationManager, createMemoryConversationStore } from '@orkestrel/agent'\r\n *\r\n * const store = createMemoryConversationStore()\r\n * const manager = createConversationManager({ store })\r\n * const conversation = manager.add()\r\n * conversation.add({ role: 'user', content: 'hello' })\r\n * await manager.save(conversation.id)            // persist the conversation\r\n * ```\r\n */\r\nexport function createMemoryConversationStore(): ConversationStoreInterface {\r\n\treturn new MemoryConversationStore()\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Creates a {@link DatabaseConversationStore} over any {@link DriverInterface}, defaulting to\r\n * `createMemoryDriver()` â€” the durable, driver-pluggable backing for the conversation persistence\r\n * seam, holding each snapshot as one opaque JSON column and standing as the opt-in twin of\r\n * {@link createMemoryConversationStore}. The exact twin of\r\n * {@link import('@orkestrel/workspace').createDatabaseWorkspaceStore}.\r\n *\r\n * @remarks\r\n * Builds a one-table database (`conversations`, keyed by `id`) over the supplied driver, the snapshot\r\n * held as one opaque JSON column â€” the column map is `{ id; snapshot }` where `snapshot` is a\r\n * `rawShape` (a JSON blob), exactly as\r\n * {@link import('@orkestrel/workspace').createDatabaseWorkspaceStore} stores its snapshot. The\r\n * snapshot is already a complete, self-contained, pure-JSON payload, so storing it whole is lossless\r\n * and keeps the row type flat (the column reads back as `unknown`, narrowed on `get` by\r\n * {@link import('./validators.js').isConversationSnapshot}). The `driver` defaults to\r\n * {@link createMemoryDriver}, so the store also works in memory out of the box; pass a server\r\n * `createJSONDriver` / `createSQLiteDriver` (or a browser IndexedDB driver) for a persistent one â€”\r\n * the durability is the driver's job, the store engine is shared. It swaps in behind\r\n * {@link ConversationStoreInterface} without touching the manager or the conversation.\r\n *\r\n * @param driver - The storage backend the snapshots persist to (defaults to {@link createMemoryDriver})\r\n * @returns A {@link ConversationStoreInterface} over the driver\r\n *\r\n * @example\r\n * ```ts\r\n * import { createConversationManager, createDatabaseConversationStore } from '@orkestrel/agent'\r\n * import { createMemoryDriver } from '@orkestrel/database'\r\n *\r\n * const store = createDatabaseConversationStore(createMemoryDriver()) // a durable driver swaps in here\r\n * const manager = createConversationManager({ store })\r\n * const conversation = manager.add()\r\n * conversation.add({ role: 'user', content: 'hello' })\r\n * await manager.save(conversation.id)            // persist the conversation (one JSON column)\r\n * ```\r\n */\r\nexport function createDatabaseConversationStore(\r\n\tdriver: DriverInterface = createMemoryDriver(),\r\n): ConversationStoreInterface {\r\n\t// The snapshot is stored as one opaque JSON column (`rawShape`), so the row infers flat â€”\r\n\t// `{ id: string; `priority` orders the instruction in an\r\n * {@link InstructionManagerInterface}'s rendered list (higher first) and defaults to `0`.\r\n * Stored immutable â€” never mutated after creation.\r\n *\r\n * @param input - `name` / `content` (required) and an optional `priority` (see\r\n *   {@link InstructionInput})\r\n * @returns A working {@link InstructionInterface}\r\n *\r\n * @example\r\n * ```ts\r\n * import { createInstruction } from '@orkestrel/agent'\r\n *\r\n * const instruction = createInstruction({ name: 'tone', content: 'Be concise.', priority: 5 })\r\n * ```\r\n */\r\nexport function createInstruction(input: InstructionInput): InstructionInterface {\r\n\treturn new Instruction(input)\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Creates an instruction registry â€” an {@link InstructionManagerInterface} holding\r\n * immutable instructions keyed by `name`, listed by descending `priority`.\r\n *\r\n * @remarks\r\n * Starts empty; Checker unit A1-fix-2-check rules on the mechanical claims; its\r\nverdict is `a1-fix-2-check-verdict.md`.\r\n# Guides\r\n\r\nAn index into this repository's guides, by concept and by directory, following the\r\ndocumentation contract in [`.claude/rules/documentation.md`](../.claude/rules/documentation.md).\r\n\r\n## By concept\r\n\r\n| Concept  | Spec                         | Source                                                   | Tests                                                                            |\r\n| -------- | ---------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\r\n| Scaffold | [`scaffold.md`](scaffold.md) | [`src/core`](../src/core), [`src/server`](../src/server) | [`tests/src/core`](../tests/src/core), [`tests/src/server`](../tests/src/server) |\r\n\r\n`scaffold.md` documents the union of the package's library faces: the pure core\r\n([`src/core`](../src/core)) and the server face ([`src/server`](../src/server)). The server face\r\ncarries the `Materializer` that writes a target, the `Upstream` reader that fetches releases and\r\nguide mirrors, and the `WriteTransaction` those writes stage through. The `scaffold` executable\r\n([`src/bin`](../src/bin)) publishes no barrel, so it is documented in prose and sits outside the\r\nsurface bijection.\r\n\r\nThat bijection is the row's contract, and [`tests/guides.test.ts`](../tests/guides.test.ts)\r\nenforces it: every symbol the guide documents exists in the core barrel or the server barrel, and\r\nevery symbol either barrel exports is documented.\r\n\r\n## By directory\r\n\r\n| Directory    | Guide                        |\r\n| ------------ | ---------------------------- |\r\n| `src/core`   | [`scaffold.md`](scaffold.md) |\r\n| `src/server` | [`scaffold.md`](scaffold.md) |\r\n| `src/bin`    | [`scaffold.md`](scaffold.md) |\r\n\r\n## Line reference\r\n\r\nThis repo vendors a byte-identical guide mirror for **every published `@orkestrel/*` package**,\r\nnot only its own dependencies. Scaffold is the line's blueprint compiler: `new` seeds a\r\nworkspace's vendored dependency guides, and `catalog` refreshes them â€” the declared set by\r\ndefault, or the complete published line under `--all`. Each mirror is fetched from its own\r\nrepository's `main` at `guides/<name>.md`.\r\n\r\nThese subsets carry extra weight:\r\n\r\n- **Runtime dependencies** â€” `@orkestrel/console` ([`console.md`](console.md)),\r\n  `@orkestrel/contract` ([`contract.md`](contract.md)), `@orkestrel/emitter`\r\n  ([`emitter.md`](emitter.md)), `@orkestrel/markdown` ([`markdown.md`](markdown.md)),\r\n  `@orkestrel/process` ([`process.md`](process.md)), and `@orkestrel/template`\r\n  ([`template.md`](template.md)). The library faces reach contract, emitter, and template; the\r\n  `scaffold` executable reaches console, contract, markdown, and process.\r\n- **Development** â€” `@orkestrel/guide` ([`guide.md`](guide.md)) powers this repository's\r\n  guides-parity suite, [`tests/guides.test.ts`](../tests/guides.test.ts).\r\n\r\nEvery mirror documents **that package's** surface, not anything sourced in this repo. A mirror\r\nthat drifts from its upstream `main` is a defect: refresh it rather than editing it here.\r\n\r\n## See also\r\n\r\n- [`AGENTS.md`](../AGENTS.md) and [`.claude/rules/documentation.md`](../.claude/rules/documentation.md) â€” the repository rules and documentation contract.\r\n8:Validation is where untrusted data — an HTTP body, a parsed JSON blob, a tool argument — crosses into typed code. This module is that crossing: guards turn `unknown` into a narrowed `T` without leaking a hostile input's throw, while parsers return a typed value or `undefined` for readable invalid input. Here a **reader** is a public operation whose documented result depends on inspecting a caller-owned container, and an **advertised read** is the exact property, key, element, iteration, or reflection that operation says it observes. REQUIRED readers refuse an incomplete advertised read with a coded `ContractError`; unreadability never becomes their absence or accept-anything answer. Total guards instead answer `false`; optional lookup/coercion readers (`enumerableKeys`, `resolveField`, `parseArray`, `parseEnum`, and the `parse*Field` family) deliberately answer `undefined`. Schema inversion widens readable unsupported, depth-exhausted, or cyclic nodes to `rawShape({})`, but a traversal that fails is refused as unreadable rather than widened. **READABLE** therefore means every advertised read for the operation being discussed completes; **STABLE** means those observable reads also keep the same answers across separate calls. The module deliberately ships **flat primitives** instead of a full schema framework — every guard is a one-argument total function and parsers coerce-or-bail rather than collect errors. The `parseJSON` / `parseJSONAs` text boundary stays lazy by default; `isJSONValue`, `parseJSONValue`, `cloneJSONValue`, and the record-root `cloneJSONRecord` are explicit deep whole-tree operations. Recursive contracts remain opt-in through the shape DSL, where the tree is finite and developer-authored. Its source is [`src/core`](../src/core), surfaced through the `@src/core` barrel.\n10:## Surface\n30:### Primitive & null-ish guards\n53:### Structural & collection guards\n73:#### Recognizing a plain record\n79:### Array & typed-array guards\n98:### Emptiness guards\n113:### Function & constructor guards\n128:### Combinators\n169:### Parsers\n194:### JSON\n196:The safe JSON surface keeps text parsing lazy: `parseJSON` returns `unknown`, while `parseJSONAs` walks only the guard shape supplied by its caller. `isJSONValue` and `parseJSONValue` are shipped explicit deep whole-tree gates; `isBoundedJSONValue` adds the fixed resource boundary, and `isBoundedJSONRecord` adds the record-root invariant. `JSONRecord` and `cloneJSONRecord` provide the record-root ownership contract required by metadata and persistence consumers, while `cloneJSONValue` owns any JSON root. A dedicated `JSONArray` alias, broad deep `isJSONObject` / `isJSONSchema` validators, and the ~50-field `JSONSchemaDefinition` remain deliberately omitted. Compose narrower shapes from the combinators and read a parsed blob field-by-field with the `parse*Field` readers when whole-tree work is unnecessary.\n219:### Helper\n268:`matchesJSONDepth` is deliberately depth-only: active cycles terminate successfully at the depth layer but remain invalid JSON, honest sparse holes add no child depth but fail the dense `isJSONValue` contract, and readable exotics pass as leaves but fail JSON validity. `isBoundedJSONValue` therefore performs two total sequential observations — fixed depth first, then the existing JSON guard — rather than promising an atomic snapshot of caller-owned state. It validates but does not own; use `cloneJSONValue` / `cloneJSONRecord` when an independent frozen snapshot is required. The fixed cap is package safety mechanism, not configurable product policy: byte budgets, key budgets, configured wire depth, unsafe-name rejection, descriptor policy, and application-specific limits remain with their consuming package.\n270:### Membership\n280:### Threat model\n291:- **Published snapshots are frozen and faithful.** A builder freezes what it returns and copies what you hand it; `cloneShape` / `cloneSchema` / `cloneJSONValue` publish deeply frozen graphs that retain no caller reference, and a snapshot that cannot be faithful refuses instead of normalizing.\n294:- **A shared reference costs one visit, a published bound, or nothing at all.** `COMPILE_DEPTH_LIMIT`, `GUARD_DEPTH_LIMIT`, `FAULT_LIMIT`, `INFER_DEPTH_LIMIT`, `INFER_BREADTH_LIMIT`, `CLONE_NODE_LIMIT` and `COMPILE_NODE_LIMIT` are the published bounds, and where a bound is the wrong instrument the walk carries an identity memo instead. Sharing arrives on BOTH sides and both are answered: `COMPILE_NODE_LIMIT` caps what a shared-child DECLARATION expands into (see Compilers), and `is`, `audit` and `explain` answer a shared-reference VALUE in one visit per (compiled node, object) pair however many paths reach that object. Both halves are needed: without the value-side memo an ordinary graph with several references per level — a few hundred bytes, no attacker, shared references are normal data — costs node reads exponential in its depth. A faulted node is re-walked at its new path, because a fault carries one, and stays bounded by `FAULT_LIMIT`. `cloneJSONValue` and `parse` deliberately still expand and each says so here: `cloneJSONValue` duplicates an alias by contract and caps that at `CLONE_NODE_LIMIT`, and `parse` materializes a tree — see the line below. The claim is about those doors, not about a guard a caller composes: nesting `arrayOf` twenty levels deep builds a walker of your own, and it pays per path.\n296:What is NOT guaranteed, beyond the realm assumption above: a value whose observable reads CHANGE between two calls can leave any two-call law unsatisfied (see Domains), `Date` and `undefined` fall outside the round-trip law, and `schemaToShape`'s memo is ancestor-context-sensitive across graphs (see its row). Two more follow from the memo above and are named rather than left to be discovered. `parse` returns a TREE, so it pays for the tree its input expands into: eighteen shared arrays parse into 262,143 of them in about 370 ms, where `is`, `audit` and `explain` answer the same value in under a millisecond and `cloneJSONValue` refuses that exact graph at `CLONE_NODE_LIMIT`. That cost is the RESULT rather than the walk, which is why it carries no cap — a cap here would refuse a value the caller asked to be handed — so measure an untrusted graph's expansion before parsing it. And within ONE call, `is`, `audit` and `explain` read a given object once per compiled node rather than once per path, so a value whose reads change BETWEEN two reads of the same node can answer differently than it did when every path re-read it. Both are consequences of trading repeated reads for bounded work, and the trade is stated so it can be checked rather than discovered. A third limit belongs to the guards you compose YOURSELF: the memo above lives in a compiled artifact, so a chain you build by hand out of combinators — `arrayOf(arrayOf(...))` and its siblings — has no shared ledger between its independently constructed links and re-reads a shared object once per PATH. Measured: a depth-18 hand-composed chain over an 18-level shared graph answers in about 250 ms, growing fourfold every two levels, where the same value through `createContract` answers in under a millisecond. Compile the declaration when the value may share references; the compiled route is the one this bound covers.\n298:### Types\n334:### Classes\n342:### `ContractError`\n348:These refusal families may throw, and whenever they do they throw only this class: REQUIRED READS (`readValue` and the public inferer/helper/combinator/compiler readers layered over it — normally `structure` with `<reader>: <subject> could not be read`, while `RegExp` readers use `pattern`), shape CONSTRUCTION (every builder validates every runtime argument position before returning — `bound` / `range` / `empty` / `placement` / `pattern` / `literal` / `structure`), CLONING (`cloneJSONValue` / `cloneJSONRecord` for inexact JSON data, cycles, or hostile traversal; `cloneSchema` / `cloneShape` / `ownShape`, and `rawShape` through its snapshot — `clone` plus declaration-policy codes), VALIDATION (`validateShape` and `ShapeValidator` — `range` / `empty` / `placement` / `structure` / `literal` / `cycle` / `bound` / `pattern`), the COMPILATION gate (every `compile*` export plus `createContract` routes through that same `validateShape` — the same declaration-policy codes, plus `depth`), and GENERATION (`compileGenerator` on an unsatisfiable request — `generate`; `drawRandom` on a broken sample source — `random`). Every message opens with the reader that OWNS the rule it enforces, never the engine that happened to run it. A declaration rule belongs to the shared gate and reads `validateShape: …` wherever it is applied, including inside `ShapeCloner`, which enforces the same rules while capturing; `cloneShape: …` is reserved for the ownership rules the cloner itself owns — own data discriminants, inherited fields, accessors, read stability, unreadable property maps, and a failed snapshot; and `ShapeCloner.clone: …`, `SchemaCloner.clone: …`, `JSONCloner.clone: …` and `ShapeValidator.validate: …` name a refusal about the CALL rather than the declaration, such as reentry. One rule therefore has one diagnostic at each of those doors: `cloneShape`, `ownShape`, `validateShape`, `ShapeValidator`, every `compile*` export and `createContract` report the same malformed declaration with the same code and the same message, across every declaration-policy family the gate owns (`placement`, `range`, `bound`, `pattern`, `empty`, `literal`, `cycle`, `depth`, `structure`). That is a statement about those doors and those families, enforced by a sweep rather than asserted: it says nothing about a door outside the list. `createContract` observes its caller’s source twice — once as the declaration, once while cloning it — so a LIVE declaration that changes between those walks can be refused by ownership rather than by the gate, and each refusal names the boundary that owns the rule it broke. An error adopted by identity from another engine keeps the prefix that engine gave it. Hand-authored string declarations use the same unflagged-pattern policy as builders: a stable genuine `RegExp` carrying flags is a `pattern` refusal, and inline pattern constructs are the supported alternative. Total guards and optional readers use their non-throwing outcomes; `compileReporter` contains its diagnostic walk, while required `parse`/`audit` reads and schema inversion refuse traversal failure. Root ownership, including the frozen-state probe itself, is contained before standalone compilation begins: a revoked `Proxy`, throwing getter, or caller-thrown value becomes a coded `clone` / `structure` `ContractError`. Malformed containers, hostile proxies, and wrong primitives at any builder position become coded `ContractError` values. Every public door that can refuse runs its whole body through `contain`, which republishes anything that is not this class under the door's own name with the exact thrown value as `cause`; a door whose body cannot throw carries no boundary, because wrapping code that cannot fail misreports where the refusals are. A boundary placed per STATEMENT is only ever as complete as the last sweep, while a boundary at the door covers whatever the body reaches, enumerated or not. Its limits are named rather than promised away. A boundary covers a BODY, so it cannot reach a parameter-default initializer, which is evaluated in the function environment before the first statement runs: the one such default this package had (`compileGenerator`'s wall-clock seed) was moved into the contained body, and any future computed default belongs there too. And a boundary is not a fidelity guarantee: containment answers what a door may THROW, while what a door PUBLISHES under a redirect that lies instead of throwing is the separate job of `INTRINSICS`, the module-scope membership functions layered over it, and the indexed publication walks. Every such site is reached from the captured table — which is a claim a sweep checks, not a claim this sentence makes.\n373:### Cloners\n375:`JSONCloner` is the public state-owning JSON engine. Its constructor only retains the source; the first `clone()` performs the iterative snapshot and settles once. A successful instance replays the exact same deeply frozen root without another source read, while a failed instance releases partial traversal working state and rethrows its exact class-owned `ContractError` without another read, retaining the source and exact error for replay. That terminal settlement is nonredirectable, which needs each of these: its ownership record and error construction dispatch only through intrinsics captured while the module evaluated, AND every optional diagnostic option is read as an own property, so a construction never consults a prototype chain the caller can write. Neither replacing an intrinsic after construction nor installing a `cause`, `context` or `path` accessor on `Object.prototype` can make the first call escape with a raw value or make the replay disagree with it — including when the caller arms the pollution from inside its own reflective trap, after the walk has begun. A redirected working-state member is contained instead, and settles as an ordinary owned refusal. Active reentry permanently poisons nested, outer, and later calls with one cause-free `JSONCloner.clone: JSON cloning may not be reentered` error (`clone`, `{ shape: 'json' }`), even when hostile source code catches the nested throw. Distinct instances are independent. `cloneJSONValue` constructs a fresh instance on every call, so every eager call re-observes its source and produces a distinct composite root or distinct diagnostic when identity is observable.\n403:| `cloneJSONValue`   | function | Deep-clones exact JSON data into an owned frozen snapshot.                                     |\n439:### Shape builders\n492:### Shape types\n530:### Compilers\n600:### Inferers\n660:### Reporting\n687:#### How a union is audited and reported\n695:#### Supplying a rebuilt pattern\n703:### Domains\n736:## Methods\n740:#### `JSONClonerInterface`\n746:#### `SchemaClonerInterface`\n752:#### `ShapeClonerInterface`\n769:#### `ShapeValidatorInterface`\n781:##### Diagnostic precedence and reentry\n789:#### `ContractInterface`\n798:## Contract\n810:The `parseJSON` / `parseJSONAs` text boundary is **lazy by default**: keep the result unknown, validate only a supplied shape, or read fields individually. Whole-tree work is explicit through the shipped `isJSONValue`, fixed-cap `isBoundedJSONValue` / `isBoundedJSONRecord`, `parseJSONValue`, `cloneJSONValue`, and record-root `cloneJSONRecord`; `JSONRecord` is the reusable record-root type. A dedicated `JSONArray` alias and broad deep JSON-Schema validators / `JSONSchemaDefinition` remain omitted; compose any narrower contract a consumer actually needs.\n812:## Patterns\n814:### Capturing synchronous outcomes losslessly\n835:### Narrowing `unknown`\n850:### Composing with `recordOf` / `arrayOf` / `unionOf`\n881:### Accepting foreign interface implementations with `objectOf`\n900:### Recursive guards with `lazyOf`\n914:### Guards narrow, parsers coerce\n929:### Parsing JSON safely\n959:### Checking structural JSON safely\n969:### Declaring a shape\n999:### Validating a live shape source\n1019:### Compiling a contract\n1042:### Auditing an undeclared key\n1092:### From an existing API/DB to an MCP tool\n1157:### Practices\n1165:- **JSON text parsing is lazy by default.** Use `parseJSONAs` with a composed guard or read a `parseJSON` result field-by-field when that is sufficient. Choose `isJSONValue`, fixed-cap `isBoundedJSONValue` / `isBoundedJSONRecord`, `parseJSONValue`, `cloneJSONValue`, or record-root `cloneJSONRecord` only when an explicit deep whole-tree guard, bounded guard, parser, or owned snapshot is required. `JSONRecord` serves real record-root consumers; a dedicated `JSONArray` alias and broad deep JSON-Schema validation remain outside the surface.\n1167:## Tests\n1169:- [`tests/guides.test.ts`](../tests/guides.test.ts) — the `## Surface` ↔ `src/core` bijection (value and type exports), the `## Methods` ↔ implementing-class bijection read from the real prototypes, and the equality gate: every `Summary` cell against its declaration's description paragraph, the titled `Compiling a contract` fence against the `@example` block of that title (pinned so the titled pair cannot be retired silently), and the README pitch against this guide's tagline. It also runs the flagship fences and asserts the values their comments claim.\n1190:## See also\n2153:export interface ProviderRequest {\n2210:\tbody(request: ProviderRequest): object\n2233:export type RelayFrame =\n2243:export interface RelayOptions {\n2251:export interface RelayStreamOptions {\n2253:\treadonly request: ProviderRequest\n2265:export interface TextRead {\n/**\r\n * Reads a UTF-8 prefix of a byte stream and cancels its remainder.\r\n *\r\n * @remarks\r\n * `limit` bounds bytes passed to the decoder, including a partial final character.\r\n * An omitted limit reads to completion. Write version direction as \"earlier\" and \"later\" in public TSDoc and in the `guides/scaffold.md` guide, keeping each summary cell equal to its doc block.\r\n# Agent\r\n\r\n> The conversation runtime for the `@orkestrel` line: a pluggable `ProviderInterface`\r\n> inference boundary, the conversation layer that feeds it â€” messages, compaction,\r\n> instructions, scopes, and prompt assembly â€” and the bounded context â†’ provider â†’ tools â†’\r\n> repeat loop that carries a turn to its end.\r\n\r\nAn agent is a conversation with a model and the loop that carries it forward. A `Conversation` holds the history â€” a live tail of immutable messages plus the sections older turns were compacted into. An `AgentContext` assembles that history into the next prompt, folding in the instructions and the active workspace and applying the active scope. An `Agent` drives that prompt through a provider, dispatches whatever tools the model asks for, feeds the results back, and repeats until the model stops. Everything else in this module either configures those nouns or observes them. Source: [`src/core`](../src/core). Published through `@orkestrel/agent`.\r\n\r\nHand a provider a conversation and get back one assembled `ProviderResult` (`generate`), or a live stream of channel-tagged `ProviderDelta`s that returns that same assembled result when it ends (`stream`). Reasoning separation, the authority gate, and durable jobs sit around that boundary. The model itself is the one thing this package does not supply: `ProviderInterface` is a contract, not an implementation, so any backend that satisfies it drops in unchanged and the host application decides which one. Nor is there hidden global state, a plugin lifecycle, a prompt-template DSL, or an implicit memory store. This is a kit of composable primitives: the loop is the convenient way to use them, not the only one, and a caller that would rather bound and drive a provider by hand can skip it entirely.\r\n\r\nTools and files are borrowed, not owned. Callable tools come from [`@orkestrel/tool`](tool.md): the loop advertises their definitions to the model, dispatches the calls that come back, and feeds each `ToolResult` in as a tool message. A tool is loop machinery â€” it is never rendered into the prompt. Documents come from [`@orkestrel/workspace`](workspace.md): the context renders the active workspace into every turn, split by carrier â€” text as fenced reference blocks in the system message, images attached to the last user turn. That split is this package's own product policy, decided here because only the prompt-assembly layer knows what a turn looks like.\r\n\r\nA turn is bounded and always terminates. One `AbortSignal` â€” a cancel, a [timeout](timeout.md), and a [budget](budget.md) folded together through `AbortSignal.any` â€” bounds the whole run, and tool iteration is capped at `limit`. A cancel is not an error: it commits a partial `AgentResult` that resolves, so only a genuine provider or tool failure rejects. `generate` and `stream` share one private run, so the one-shot result can never diverge from the live stream, and a buggy observer cannot corrupt either, because the emitter isolates a listener's throw.\r\n\r\n## Surface\r\n\r\nThe agent-owned surface: the inference boundary, the conversation layer, the context and its managers, the loop, the authority gate, and the durable-job bridge. Tool and workspace entities belong to their originating packages and are consumed directly â€” never re-exported here â€” and the concrete `ProviderInterface` implementation belongs to the host application.\r\n\r\nA provider turns a conversation (plus optional tools) into a turn: `generate` resolves the assembled `ProviderResult` (content + any tool calls + any usage); `stream` yields channel-tagged `ProviderDelta`s as they arrive (`content` for answer text, `thinking` for live reasoning) and returns the same assembled result when the stream completes, so a caller can render tokens / reasoning live and still get the full outcome. Both bound the call with an `AbortSignal`:\r\n\r\n```ts\r\nimport { createAbort } from '@orkestrel/abort'\r\nimport type { ProviderInterface } from '@orkestrel/agent'\r\n\r\ndeclare const provider: literal segment (`/users`), a `:name` param\r\n(one segment), or a final `*name` wildcard (captures the rest of the path).\r\n# NDJSON\r\n\r\n> A stateful newline-delimited-JSON (NDJSON) stream parser: a self-contained handle\r\n> that turns string chunks into the complete records decoded so far, and never throws\r\n> on a malformed, blank, or non-record line.\r\n\r\n`parse(chunk)` appends `chunk` to an internal buffer and splits it on `\\n`: every\r\nline before the last is `\\n`-terminated, hence complete, and is parsed to a record,\r\nwhile the final segment is held back for the next call, so a line split across chunk\r\nboundaries is reassembled the moment its closing `\\n` arrives. Each trimmed line is\r\nfiltered â€” a blank or whitespace-only line (including one whose only content was a\r\nCRLF's trailing `\\r`) is skipped, malformed JSON is skipped silently, and a\r\nnon-record value (an array, a primitive, `null`) is dropped â€” so only plain records\r\ncome back, and a line the stream never terminates is never emitted even when the\r\nbuffered text already happens to be valid JSON. `clear()` drops the buffered partial\r\nline so a handle can be reused for a fresh stream. Nothing else is wired in: no\r\nEmitter, no server, HTTP, or agent coupling. Pair it with a streaming `TextDecoder`\r\nwhen reading a byte stream â€” the decoder handles partial characters, the parser\r\nhandles partial lines. The buffer has no size limit, so a caller fronting an\r\nuntrusted or unbounded upstream must enforce its own byte cap before feeding chunks\r\nin. Source: [`src/core`](../src/core). Surfaced through the `@src/core` barrel.\r\n\r\n## Surface\r\n\r\nCreate a parser and feed it chunks as they arrive; each `parse(chunk)`\r\nreturns the records completed so far, and a trailing partial line is held\r\nfor the next call:\r\n\r\n```ts\r\nimport { createNDJSONParser } from '@orkestrel/ndjson'\r\n\r\nconst parser = createNDJSONParser()\r\nparser.parse('{\"a\":1}\\n{\"b\"') // [{ a: 1 }] - the second line is still partial\r\nparser.parse(':2}\\n') // [{ b: 2 }] - the split line reassembled\r\nparser.clear() // drop any buffered partial - ready for a fresh stream\r\n```\r\n\r\n### Types\r\n\r\nA `Shape` cell holds an interface's data members as bare names in braces, `?`\r\nmarking an optional member and `plus` introducing its call-signature members,\r\nand a type alias's own type literal with a union's arms escaped as `\\|`.\r\n\r\n| Type                    | Kind      | Shape                  | Summary                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |\r\n| ----------------------- | --------- | ---------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\r\n| `NDJSONParserInterface` | interface | `{} plus parse, clear` | Represents the stateful NDJSON (newline-delimited JSON) stream-parser contract a consumer holds â€” a `parse` that turns each string chunk into the complete records decoded so far, and a `clear` that drops the buffered partial line. |\r\n\r\nTypes a helper's parameter as `NDJSONParserInterface` and returns the
